產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 21 日

1 群益投顧 
台玻 (1802 TT ) 10/20/2026 首戰台灣電路板產業國際博覽會 (TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來源。
Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加 。
當前營收比重為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。鑒於 Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳，
Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐步提升下，毛利表現亦將持續走揚。產品供需結構尚處緊缺階
段， 公司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價 。 給 予 投 資 建 議Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

研 究 員 ： 蔡 季 衡   
A52320@cim.capital.com.tw

台 玻 首 戰 TPCA Show ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 ， 包 括 TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 、 TS(Low 
CTE) 、 Q (Q 布 )等 ， 並 詳 解 各 產 品 特 性 及 運 用 範 疇 。 
一、 聚 焦 於 Low Dk 、 Low CTE 玻纖布 ：  
TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 具 低 介 電 損 耗 並 於 維 持 一 定 電 性 水 準 下 ， 提
供 較 佳 產 品 穩 定 性 。 目 前 主 要 應 用 於 電 子 產 業 及PCB 產 業 、IC 載 板 、 先 進
封 裝 材 料 、 航 空 及 汽 車 領 域 等 。 
TS(Low CTE) 具 低 熱 膨 脹 係 數 特 性， 並 具 備 高機 械 韌 性(剛性 )、 絕 佳 電 性 絕
緣 及 先 進 樹 酯兼 容 性。目 前 主 要 應 用 於 5G/6G 、微 波 通 訊 設 備、高 速 電 路 板、
高頻 PCB 、 光 模 塊 、 高 速 交 換 器 及資 料 傳 輸 等。  
二 、 目 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE ：  
E 布 轉 至 中 國 產 銷 ，以 Low Dk 1/Dk2 為 當 前 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來 源 。
Low CTE 則 已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前產 品 出 貨 量 已 較 2026 年初
顯 著 增 加，預 期 營 收 佔 比 將 於 2027 年 陸 續 提 升，並 於 2028 年 伴隨 近 年 陸 續
擴建 之 新 產 能 開 出 後 ，顯 著 提 高 。鑒 於 目 前 下 游 終 端 需 求 仍 以 Low Dk 產品
為主力， 研究部 預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營 收 比 重 仍 將 高 於 Low Dk1 及 Low 
CTE 。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 另 外 ， 台 玻 目 前 亦嘗
試 生 產 測 試 Low Dk3( 三代布 )， 後 續 若 生 產 良 率 過 關 ， 亦 將送 樣 驗 證 。 
三 、 不 急 於 產 品 漲 價 ， 堅 守 優 質 客 戶 連 結：  
台 玻 生 產 研 發 玻 纖 布數 十 年，始 終 秉 持 於 維 繫 良 好 客 戶 關 係，並 以 客 戶 需 求
為 營 運 宗 旨 。 雖 2025~2026 年以來 E 布 報 價 倍 數 增 長 ， 惟 基 於多年來與 客
戶 合作 建立 之 信任 及當前產品利潤貢獻尚屬可控範圍，故而 預期將 不急於
大 幅 反 映 產 品 報 價 ， 但 亦 不 排 除 部 分 調 整 產 品 報 價 。 
四 、 具 備 Q 布 生 產 技 術 及 能 力：  
台 玻 於 本 次 TPCA Show 上 ， 已 展 出 Q 布 玻 纖 紗 ，顯示 其 Q 布 技 術 儲 備 ， 且
已 具 備 接 單 出 貨 能 力 。 惟 觀 察 AI 伺 服 器 規 格 調 整，預期 2027 年 產 品 需 求
仍以 Low Dk 系 列 玻 纖 布 為 主 ， 大 量 搭 載 Q 布 尚 早 。 不 同 材 料 規 格 之 玻 纖
布 ， 其 製 程 方 式雖 雷 同 ， 惟 配 方 均 有 差 異 ，窯 爐 使 用 亦 不 同 。 因 此 ， 台 玻 將
視 下 游 廠 商 產 品 需 求 ， 決 議 Q 布 生 產 時 程。

2 群益投顧 
五、 研 究 部 預 估台玻 2027 年稅後 EPS 4.37 元 ：  
台玻 09/2026 營收 49.68 億 元 ，MoM+8.18% 、YoY+34.11% ，主因 Low Dk1/Low 
Dk2 等 高 階 玻 纖 布 出 貨 動 能 較 08/2026 提 升。若 以 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 40~45%，
且高階玻纖布佔其中 40~42% 推估， 09/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻約
MoM+7~9% 。 3Q26 營收 合計 約 141.41 億 元 ， QoQ+17.24% 、 YoY+31.82% ，
亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 138.80 億元， QoQ+15.08% 、 YoY+29.40% 。  
展望 2027 ~2028 年 ， 預 期 2027 年 Low Dk1/Dk2 玻 纖 布 出 貨 量 仍 將 較 2026
年 顯 著 提 升，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 亦 將 持 續 走 揚，2028 年 伴 隨 新 產 能 開 出 後，
有望趕上 Low Dk2 。 目 前 台 玻 Low CTE 產 品 已 陸 續 取 得 國 內 外 大 廠 認 證 ，
訂 單 能 見 度 亦 已 達 2027 年，且 2028 年 訂 單 洽 談 順 暢。因 此，供 應 鏈 產 品 需
求若 無 較 大 變 化 ，2028 年 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 仍 將持 續 成 長 。 
日本大廠 Nittobo 多 次 公 開 表 示 ， 雖 2028 年產能 倍增 ，惟 T 布 市 場 需 求 已
非 當 前 公 司 擴 產 速 度 所 能 消 化 ， 顯 示 產 品 需 求 強 勁 。 研 究 部預 估 台 玻 4Q26
底 ~2027 年 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 有 望 超 越 平 板 玻 璃 和 玻 璃 器 皿 。預估 2027 年營
收 649.42 億 元 ， YoY +26.21 %， 毛利率 32.01% ， 稅 後 淨 利 127.17 億 元 ，
YoY +90.06 %， 稅後 EPS 4.37 元 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首 戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營
收 來源。 Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較
2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 鑒於
Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳 ，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐 步 提 升 下 ， 毛 利 表 現
亦 將 持 續 走 揚。產 品 供 需 結 構 尚 處 緊 缺 階 段，公 司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價。給
予 投 資 建 議 Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

2026年 10月 30日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月雇用統計 
失業率は2.4％と前月から0.1％pt低下し、就業拡大が進展

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲
0.1％pt）し、7月の水準に戻った。失業者数は「自発的な離職」を中心に 2カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲10万人）し、就業者数は2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参
加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月から改善したとみられる。 
◼ 2026年9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と3カ月ぶり
に上昇した。新規求職申込件数が大きく減少したことで、新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同
＋0.13pt）と上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢をめぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

9月の完全失業率：2.4％と前月から低下、「自発的な離職」による失業者が大きく減少 
2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲0.1％pt）
し、7月の水準に戻った（図表2左上） 。失業者数は2カ月ぶりに減少（同▲10万人）し、就業
者数は 2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月
から改善したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差▲13万人）が大きく減少し、全体
を押し下げた。「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」（同▲1万人）は小幅に減少した。他方、「定
年又は雇用契約の満了」（同＋1万人）と「新たに求職」（同＋1万人）は小幅に増加した（巻末
の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化を業種別に見ると、「製造業」と「医療，福祉」が大きく増加し、
全体を押し上げた。「卸売業，小売業」と「情報通信業」も増加した。他方、「建設業」やその
他の非製造業は減少した。（図表2左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から 22万人増加した。
2026年春頃から増加傾向にあり、ピークだった2025年 11月の水準を上回った。非正規雇用者
は 5カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋21万人）した（図表 2右下）。 
図表 2：就業者数・完全失業率（左上） 、非労働力人口・労働参加率（右上） 、 
業種別就業者数（左下） 、雇用形態別雇用者数（右下）

9月の新規求人倍率：求職が大きく減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026年 9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上
昇した。新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同＋0.13pt）と上昇した（図表3左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数は前月から横ばいだった一方、新規求人数（前月比＋
1.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲1.4％）、新規
求職申込件数（同▲4.4％）はいずれも 3カ月ぶりに減少した。求職側は 2025年春頃から持ち
直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.01倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
3カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率も 1.79倍（同＋0.11pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上昇した（図表
3右）。

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026年 9月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％ptと 6月調査から 1％pt低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％ptと更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％ptと6月調査から3％pt低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％ptだった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
株式会社ナウキャストによる「HRog賃金 Now」の求人数指数を見ると、正社員とパート・ア
ルバイトのいずれも 2026年春頃から上昇傾向にある（巻末の雇用概況③下段左）。人手不足感
の強まりを受けて、今後も労働需要は堅調に推移するとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化させ3、
幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122条に基づく措置が失効する 7月 24日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026年7月 24日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年
8月」（大和総研レポート、2026年8月 24日）を参照。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

We hosted EMC’s 3Q26 post result call on October 29th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) EMC mentioned its proactive capacity expansion plans in coming 
years (capacity will go up to 15.25mn sheets/month by end of 2030 
vs. 5.85mn sheets/month now) are still not enough to support all the 
LTA they signed for the next 4 years, implying a solid demand 
outlook without much risk on oversupply.  
(2) In terms of the CCL pricing, the company mentioned it’s doing 
the second round of the high-end CCL (M7+) pricing hike this year, 
with 5-20% pricing hike ratio, which not only on M7/M8, but also on 
M8+/M9 grade CCL.  
(3) For 4Q26, EMC mentioned the conservative estimate on revenue 
growth rate should be at least 15-20% QoQ, with GM could at least 
expand to 38.0%-39.5% (note that the company estimated 3Q rev 
could grow 15% QoQ and GM could be 33.5%-36.5% back to 2Q26 
result call - here, but the actual outcome was revenue up 29% QoQ 
and GM hit 37.7%).  
(4) For substrate material, the company mentioned it is focusing on 
the high-end ABF substrate core-layer CCL (BT type, but for ABF 
substrate), and will hold 50% market share for the MTK TPU v8 ABF 
substrate. EMC also mentioned that it is now working with several 
customers on high end ABF used CCL qualification projects which 
the company expects to start shipment from 4Q27. 
(5) For the substrate material capacity, EMC now holds 300k 
sheets/month capacity, but will expand the capacity to at least 
1.2mn sheets/month by end of 2027. EMC mentioned the proactive 
expansion is their strategy on always prepare capacity ahead of 
customers’ demand, and mentioned these new capacity can also be 
used to produce high-end M9/M10/M11 CCL if the demand cannot 
fill all the capacity. 
2383.TW 12m	Price	Target:	 NT$11,700.00 Price:	NT$5,010.00 Upside:	 133.5%
Elite Material (2383.TW) 
EMC call takeaways: Solid demand outlook will continue all the way to 2030, with 
more earnings’ upside from new substrate CCL business

### Page 3

(6) In terms of the profitability of the substrate material, the company mentioned the
GM of this product will be at least 50%+, despite its relatively competitive pricing
compares to the market leader who has 65%+ GM on substrate material.
(7) EMC mentioned it does see AI customers start qualifying second or third sources to
improve the stability of the supply chain, which should give more room for competitors
to enter the market. However, the company continues to target 50% AI CCL market

share in the long term, and believes its best in class product quality and deep
cooperation with PCB/CSP customers can see it continue to hold the leadership position
in this high-end industry.
Investment view - read across for high-end CCL players
We maintain our positive view on the high-end CCL players, including EMC and TUC,
while we see both companies are enjoying not only favorable pricing conditions on
high-end CCL (due to the wide supply demand gap) but also the accelerating product

mix improvement speed (we expect M9 massive adoption to start from 2H27 with 50%+
adoption rate for next generation AI server, and expect EMC to start M10 CCL product
shipment from 2H27 with mass volume shipment to start from 2028). See our Oct 28
report.
Both EMC and TUC mentioned the proactive capacity expansion they are planning in the
next 3-5 years (both companies will have 150-250% more capacity in 2029-2030 vs.
today) are still not enough to support the high-end AI/LEO/switching customers’

demand, suggesting an even more favorable pricing outlook. We expect the high-end
CCL pricing will go up by 40%+ YoY in 2027 considering the increasing tightness
condition of the high-end CCL industry (vs. 20-30% YoY 2026), which should drive both
company’s GM further in coming years.
In terms of competition, while we already model EMC’s AI CCL market share to decease
from 60-70% in 2026 to ~50% in the next three years, we do believe EMC’s earnings will

grow by 90% 2026-29 CAGR even with this 50% market share. For TUC, we expect it
should be the key share gainer from EMC considering CSPs duo source policy (we think
TUC would be a strong option due to not only its better technology skillset but also its
proactive expansion plans while other high-end CCL players are not expanding capacity
or only expanding slightly on the capacity), and we expect the company will have
multiple AI customers including AI ASIC/GPU end customers from 2H27.

Overall, we are even more positive on EMC/TUC compared to 3 months ago, after seeing
customers solid plans on high-end CCL LTA and customers’ design upgrade on CCL spec
(we had expected M10 MP would happen in 2029 or beyond only). We believe
EMC/TUC’s key competition advantage is not only from the leadership position in the
high-end CCL market, but also their proactive capacity expansion plans which makes
them attractive to AI customers as most of the other high-end CCL players are not

expanding enough capacity in 2027/28.
Maintain Buy on EMC / TUC with TP of NT$11,700 / NT$3,460, implying 134%/150%
upside vs. last close.

### Page 5

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Hon Precision (7769.TW): Large package and CPO on track to drive 2027 
content growth; 3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$12,600; Buy
29 October 2026 | 3:16AM HKT
Hon Precision delivered better-than-expected 3Q26 results, and highlighted: 1) 
product mix upgrade: a higher ASP for its large-package (Mega Tray) solution, with 
20-30% of some customers’ new orders already on Mega Tray, 2) CPO Insertion 4 
customer qualification expected to finish by 2026 year-end, with mass production 
targeted in 1Q27, 3) a reiterated capacity expansion target of +50% for 2027, and 4) 
new projects expected to lift GM from 2H27. We remain positive on Hon Precision 
and raise our 12m TP to NT$12,600 (implied 49x 2027E P/E vs. 83% NI YoY in 
2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy. 
ATC platforms across FT and SLT to meet customers’ needs 
During the meeting, management laid out its ATC roadmap in detail, covering three 
platforms. ATC 3 uses liquid cooling and is adopted by an ASIC customer, with fast 
temperature ramp, c.10kW of power (vs. market mainstream of 2-3kW) and a 
25-125°C range. ATC 5 uses refrigerant and supports tri-temp (-70-125°C) at 
c.10kW, mainly for automotive customers. ATC 8 uses refrigerant with c.25kW of 
power, for high-power applications. For large packages such as 200mm x 200mm, 
management plans to handle 12kW by end-2026 and 25kW by end-2027. Overall, 
Hon offers a different ATC system for each customer’s test requirements and installs 
it on its FT or SLT handlers. Management believes this ability to meet varying 
temperature and power needs is why customers favor Hon’s products. 
Mega Tray solution across FT and SLT supports a higher ASP uplift 
The large-package (Mega Tray) solution targets end-customer packages larger than 
120mm x 150mm and is backward-compatible with current chip sizes. Small-volume 
shipments start in 2H26 and penetration is anticipated to rise in 2027. In final test 
(FT), Hon offers the HP9050, a dual-temp model, and the HP1050, a tri-temp model, 
which can be paired with different ATC platforms depending on customer needs. In 
system-level test (SLT), Hon offers the HP-3036, which supports 36 test boards in 
parallel (vs. 6-12 today), with an ASP more than 2x current, as longer test times make 
parallel testing increasingly important. Management emphasized the importance of 
integrating full automation and advanced AOI into these new handlers. The handlers 
can also be paired with Autonomous Mobile Robots (AMRs), which OSAT customers 
are actively adopting. On ASP , Mega Tray enjoys better price vs. the mainstream 
handler solution. For some customers, 20-30% of new orders are already on Mega 
Tray.

### Page 2

CPO qualification by 2026 year-end 
Management said its CPO Insertion 4 tool has been ready for mass production since 
end-October, with current shipments mainly small-volume engineering tools. Customer 
qualification starts in November and is expected to finish in December. The customer 
targets mass production in 1Q27, and Hon has prepared long lead time materials in 
advance. 
The tool tests simultaneously for optical and electrical performance. This requires two 
thermal systems, one for the ASIC and a separate system for the OEs, while ATE handles 
the electrical test. Hon’s in-house optical alignment stage is still under qualification. 
For CPC, Hon is focused on mechanical alignment. Some customers have strong demand 
and are looking to complete NPI as soon as possible. Hon is co-developing with a US 
networking customer and a major ATE vendor. 
2027 capacity expansion reiterated 
Looking into 2027, management reiterated its capacity expansion target of +50%. With 
order visibility for the next 2-3 quarters, we expect revenue to keep growing into 1H27. 
On margins, high-end ATC and Mega Tray shipments start in 4Q26, and CPO tools begin 
delivery in 1Q27. With a 3-6 month lag for equipment revenue recognition, management 
expects these new products to drive GM higher in 2H27. 
On competition, management said major OSATs currently mainly use Hon’s equipment, 
and challengers are mostly at an early stage. Management sees its advantage in ATC 
technology, where it leads peers, as well as a more complete product portfolio given 
widely varying customer requirements. On CPO, management sees its optical and 
electrical co-testing tool as next-generation equipment, leading in the market. 
Earnings revision and Valuation

3Q26 beat: Hon Precision’s 3Q26 revenue of NT$16.2bn (+17.4% QoQ, +97.5% YoY) 
came in 5.0% above GSe and 7.4% above Bloomberg consensus. Margin-wise, 3Q26 GM 
of 56.9% (vs. 56.1% in 2Q26) was in line with GSe/cons of 56.5%/57.0%. For opex, the 
company’s 3Q26 opex ratio of 7.3% was higher vs. GSe/cons of 5.6%/6.6%, primarily 
due to higher R&D expense. As a result, 3Q26 EPS of NT$37.33 came in 4.7% above GSe 
of NT$35.64 and 8.0% above cons of NT$34.61.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and raise our EPS by 2% / 2% / 1% in 
2026-28E mainly on higher revenues and GM, reflecting the better-than-expected 3Q26

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Hon Precision (7769.TW) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on Hon Precision. Our 12-month TP of NT$12,600 is based 
on a target P/E of 32x (based on close peer correlation analysis) applied to our 2028E 
EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: 
(1) 1.5x beta, (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) softer AI/HPC demand, (2) slower adoption of SLT 
among AI ASICs, and (3) intensifying competition.

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

法 說 前 瞻               產業：封測 
日月光法說會前財務預估 
4Q26 Full Process 放量帶動公司獲利持續轉佳

| 財報訊息   
日月光即將於10/29(四)舉行法說會，市場普遍預估4Q26營收有望季
增約中高個位數，且毛利率受惠於LEAP業務持續成長挹注ATM獲
利，有望進一步攀升；另關注公司對於2027年LEAP營收、ATM獲
利結構有無進一步更新。 
| 營運現況  
3Q26營收優於公司財測：3Q26營收季增27.8%至2,441.6億元，優於
公司先前財測 (估營收季增 21~22%)，主因為 ATM業務大幅優於預
期，另EMS表現也微幅優於預期，兩者成長動能中， ATM受惠於
AI/HPC需求強勁和封測報價調漲，EMS則因蘋果手機季節性備貨、
記憶體成本轉嫁之挹注。我們預估毛利率21.4%、稅後EPS 5.75元。  
| 營運展望  
Full Process 放量帶動LEAP營收、獲利進一步成長：我們預估本季
營收有望季增4.8%至2,557.6億元，約落在市場共識之下緣，主要成
長動能來自於AMD伺服器CPU Venice帶動Full Process放量，且進一
步改善ATM獲利結構。展望明年，我們觀察：(1)封測產業產能利用
率維持高檔、AI/HPC需求維持強勁，持續支撐封測報價存在漲價空
間，且明年Full Process在AMD伺服器CPU、博通交換器晶片等訂單
挹注下 ， 維持高速成長 ， 預估明年LEAP營收將由今年的37億美元成
長至74億美元以上(公司先前已提到明年LEAP營收將翻倍)；(2)EMS
雖面臨消費性電子需求不彰，但於3Q26漲價後，明年營收將有全年
之漲價貢獻。

營收走勢圖

電子–資訊服務 
藍新資訊(6938.TT) 
維運收入堆疊，AI 與資安接棒成長

研究部初次給予藍新資訊買進投資評等 ， 主要考量 ：(1)企業 AI 應用由
概念驗證走向落地，加上資安設備屆期汰換與金融業大型主機換機，專
案建置需求延續至 2027 年；(2)建置案驗收後轉入長期維護維運 ， 持續
性收入逐年堆疊，墊高獲利基礎；(3)上櫃現金增資擴大資本規模，承接
億元級大型專案的能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估藍新資訊  2026、
2027 年營收分別為 8.33 億元與 9.55 億元，分別成長 23.1% 與 
14.7% YoY；稅後 EPS 分別為 7.38 元與 8.39 元，分別成長 23.9% 與 
28.3% YoY。藍新資訊以核心系統維運掌握客戶升級需求，並以約九成盈
餘發放現金股利，以 10/16 興櫃收盤價 80.8 元計算，2026 年現金殖
利率約 7.2%。 採樣資訊服務類股本益比介於 10～20 倍 ， 考量藍新資訊 
2026、2027 年獲利維持雙位數成長，以 2027 年 EPS 為評價基礎，給
予區間中緣的 15 倍本益比，目標價 125 元，評等為買進。

1. 核心系統技術門檻高，客戶黏著度強 
藍新資訊以 IBM UNIX 大型主機起家 ， 承接金融核心交易與企業 ERP 系
統，國內具備同等能力的業者有限；建置與資料搬遷不委外原廠，使毛
利率高於一般設備經銷商，主要客戶合作多逾十年。

2. 建置帶動維運，持續性收入逐年堆疊 
核心系統驗收並度過保固期後，客戶多續簽一至三年以上的維護合約，
存續率逾九成 ， 維運收入因此逐年堆疊 ，2025 年以近四成營收貢獻逾五
成毛利 。 研究部預估整合技術與服務 2026、2027 年營收分別成長 13.4% 
與 14.5% YoY。

3. AI 落地與換機需求，上櫃後承接大型專案 
企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，帶動 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年
約 3% 升至約 8%，加上資安與大型主機換機，專案需求延續至 2027 
年。上櫃後單案規模可望擴大至 1～2 億元，研究部預估商品銷售與系
統整合合計 2026、2027 年營收分別成長 29.4% 與 14.8% YoY。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 2

公司簡介 
藍新資訊成立於 2014 年，由藍新科技的系統整合事業群分割獨立，2018 年
起獲精誠資訊策略性投資，目前精誠持股約 48%。藍新資訊為資訊服務產業
的系統整合商，向上透過加值代理商取得 IBM、HPE、Dell、Cisco、NetApp、
VMware、Palo Alto 等國際原廠軟硬體，向下直接服務政府機關、金融機構與
大型企業。藍新資訊以 IBM Power 等 UNIX 大型主機平台起家，長期承接企
業 ERP、MES、HIS 與金融核心交易系統的建置與維運，並延伸至儲存備援、
網路資安與 AI 算力平台。

產品結構 
藍新資訊的營收分為商品銷售 、 系統整合 、 整合技術與服務三大業務 。 商品銷
售與系統整合屬專案性收入，提供伺服器、儲存、網路與資安設備的銷售、規
劃與安裝建置，2025 年營收占比分別為 10.3% 與 50.7%；依應用區分，雲
端基礎建設近五成，數據儲存、數位韌性與 AI 應用平台約 25～35%，資訊
安全與網路約 17～25%。整合技術與服務為保固期滿後的例行維護、SLA 支
援、高階維運與人力派駐，屬持續性收入，2025 年營收占比 38.8%，貢獻約 
54% 的毛利 。 另有少量機臺租賃收入 （占比 0.2%） ， 圖一併入商品銷售計算。

圖一、2024～2027F 藍新資訊各業務營收比重

資料來源：藍新資訊、統一投顧預估

藍新資訊為系統整合商，以 UNIX 
大型主機技術切入政府、金融與
大型企業的核心系統，並延伸至
儲存備援、網路資安與 AI 算力
平台。

整合技術與服務以  38.8% 的營
收占比貢獻約 54% 毛利，是藍新
資訊的獲利核心。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 3

商業模式 
藍新資訊以專案建置取得客戶核心系統，再以長期維護維運把一次性收入轉
為持續性收入 。 專案完成驗收並度過保固期後 ， 客戶多延續簽訂一至三年以上
的維護合約 ； 核心系統對穩定度要求高 ， 更換服務商的風險與成本也高 ，近三
年 平 均 維 護 合 約 存 續 率 逾 九 成 ， 合 約 件 數 2023 ～2025 年複合成長率  
14.48%。 維運過程掌握客戶的 IT 環境 ， 又帶出設備汰換 、 架構升級與資安強
化的新專案 ， 形成建置帶動維運 、 維運帶動升級的循環。 整合技術與服務以同
一組技術團隊服務更多設備與合約 ， 人力成本相對固定 ， 營收占比由 2024 年
的 33.0% 提升至 2025 年的 38.8%，毛利率由 40.7% 升至 43.1%，帶動整
體毛利率由 2024 年的 28.1% 升至 2025 年的 31.0%。

圖二、藍新資訊由專案導入延伸至長期維運

資料來源：藍新資訊，統一投顧整理

營收季節性 
藍新資訊營收具季節性 ， 政府機關與企業多於年底集中驗收結案 ， 第四季為全
年高峰，2023～2025 年第四季營收分別占全年 42.7%、52.2% 與 41.2%，12 
月單月即占全年 25～31%。商品銷售與系統整合於驗收時點認列，單季營收
隨大型專案進度波動 ； 整合技術與服務依合約期間認列 ， 可平滑專案收入的波
動。2026 年 1～9 月營收 5.06 億元（+27.2% YoY），研究部預估 4Q26 營
收 3.26 億元，占全年 39.2%。

建置帶動維運 、 維運帶動升級 ， 維
護合約存續率逾九成，持續性收
入逐年堆疊並推升整體毛利率。

年底驗收結案集中，第四季營收
占全年四至五成；維運收入依合
約認列，平滑專案收入的波動。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 4

圖三、2023～2026F 藍新資訊各季營收

資料來源：藍新資訊、統一投顧預估

客戶結構與技術能量 
藍新資訊累計服務客戶逾 400 家，營收約 50% 來自民營大型企業，政府機
關約 40%，金融機構約 10%，客戶包括行政院所屬部會、金控與證券業，以及
高科技與製造業龍頭，主要客戶合作多逾十年。客戶分散，2025 年前十大客
戶合計占營收約 35%，單一最大客戶占 7.0%；1Q26 因數位轉型再造建置案
第一階段驗收，單一客戶占比升至 14.3%，屬專案認列時點造成的短期集中。
藍新資訊員工約 135 人，其中技術人員 86 人，累計取得 642 張專業證照，
其中雲端運算、AI 與大數據、資訊安全與  IBM 大型主機四大領域合計占  
93%。建置與資料搬遷由內部團隊執行、不委外原廠，可自行完成  SAP 與 
Oracle ERP 的跨平台移轉，是毛利率高於一般設備經銷商的主因。

AI 應用與資訊安全布局 
藍新資訊成立 AI 智能發展部，提供 GPU 算力平台、向量資料庫、RAG 知識
庫 、 企業私有化大型語言模型部署與資料治理 ， 並以駐點輔導與教育訓練協助
客戶落地，以既有逾 400 家客戶為交叉銷售基礎。單純銷售設備的毛利率有
限 ， 藍新資訊以自有技術團隊提供資料治理 、系統整合與後續維運 ， 聚焦整合
服務而非設備銷售 。 資安業務不與專業資安廠商正面競爭 ， 而是從既有維運客
戶切入，承接防火牆汰換、網路架構升級、零信任身分與存取控管，以及雙活
資料中心與災難備援。上櫃現金增資後，藍新資訊已開始承接 8,000 萬元以
上的案件，未來可承接 1～2 億元等級的大型專案，較過去單案多在 5,000
～6,000 萬元以下明顯擴大。

客戶逾 400 家且分散，建置與資
料搬遷不委外，是藍新資訊毛利
率高於一般設備經銷商的主因。

AI 由伺服器銷售延伸至資料治
理與應用落地，以整合服務支撐
毛利率；資安則從維運切入換機
商機。

### Page 5

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 5

產業分析 
台灣資訊服務市場 
依資策會 MIC 統計與預估，台灣系統整合市場 2023～2027 年複合成長率約 
7.8%，資訊委外市場則達 21.1%，主因企業 IT 架構日趨複雜、資訊人才短
缺，基礎設施維運與資安逐步交由專業廠商承接；台灣資安產值 2025～2027 
年亦可望維持雙位數成長 。 研究部認為委外與資安成長高於系統整合 ， 有利維
運收入占比近四成、並以維運切入資安換機的藍新資訊。

AI 落地與資安換機推升系統整合需求 
企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，除採購 GPU 伺服器，還需搭配儲存、高
速網路 、 容器平台 、 資料治理與權限控管 ， 導入 AI Agent 後亦須與既有 ERP、
人資及核心交易系統串接；台灣企業的研發與財務等敏感資料多傾向留在自
有機房，私有化 AI 部署更仰賴具備底層整合能力的系統整合商。AI 普及同
時提高資料外洩風險 ， 老舊網路與資安設備又陸續停止原廠支援 ， 政府與企業
須換機並導入零信任架構與雙活備援。核心系統亦進入換機期，金融業  IBM 
大型主機進入汰換階段，企業 ERP 平台也因效能與資安規範展開新一輪升
級。研究部認為AI 落地、資安換機與核心系統汰換同時發生，加上 IT 人才
短缺使企業提高維運委外比重，有利兼具建置與維運能力的系統整合商。

營運概況 
1. AI 基礎設施建置帶動 2Q26 營收年增逾三成 
企業加速建置基礎設施帶動 2Q26 營收達 1.85 億元（+27.3% QoQ 、+32.6% 
YoY） 。惟記憶體與關鍵零組件漲價推升設備採購成本，毛利率降至 29.2%（-
2.0ppt QoQ、-0.8ppt YoY），營益率 12.9%；稅後淨利 1,930 萬元（+5.3% 
QoQ、+118.3% YoY），EPS 1.54 元。

2. 年底驗收旺季帶動 2026 年營收年增逾兩成 
3Q26 營收 1.77 億元（-4.1% QoQ 、+38.2% YoY ），1～9 月累計營收年增 
27.2%。年底為政府與企業驗收結案高峰，政府預算解凍後標案陸續執行，預
估 4Q26 營收 3.26 億元（+84.3% QoQ、+17.1% YoY），惟大型建置案比重
提高，毛利率降至 26.6%，EPS 2.72 元。預估 2026 年營收 8.33 億元
（+23.1% YoY），EPS 7.38 元。

3. AI 與資安需求延續，2027 年營收維持雙位數成長 
展望 2027 年，企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，AI 相關營收占比已由 
2025 年約 3% 提升至約 8%，部分客戶的 AI 案件已進入概念驗證，並著手
編列 2027 年預算；老舊網路與資安設備陸續停止原廠支援，加上金融業大
型主機進入汰換期，換機需求延續，部分大型專案的認列亦延續至 2027 年。
預估 2027 年營收 9.55 億元 （+14.7% YoY） ，毛利率 29.6%，EPS 8.39 元。

台灣資訊委外與資安市場成長高
於系統整合，有利維運收入占比
近四成的藍新資訊。

AI 落地、資安換機與核心系統汰
換同時發生，系統整合商由銷售
設備轉向提供整體架構與長期維
運。

基礎設施建置帶動 2Q26 營收年
增 32.6%，惟記憶體與零組件漲
價使毛利率降至 29.2%。

年底 驗收旺季加上政府預算解
凍 ， 預估 4Q26 營收年增 17.1%，
2026 年 EPS 7.38 元。

資安與大型主機換機需求延續，
預估 2027 年營收年增 14.7%，
EPS 8.39 元。

### Page 6

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 6

投資建議 
研究部初次給予藍新資訊買進投資評等，主要考量：(1)企業 AI 應用由概念
驗證走向落地 ， 加上資安設備屆期汰換與金融業大型主機換機 ， 專案建置需求
延續至 2027 年；(2)建置案驗收後轉入長期維護維運 ， 持續性收入逐年堆疊 ，
墊高獲利基礎；(3)上櫃現金增資擴大資本規模，承接億元級大型專案的能力
提升。綜上所述，研究部預估藍新資訊 2026、2027 年營收分別為 8.33 億
元與 9.55 億元，分別成長 23.1% 與 14.7% YoY ；稅後 EPS 分別為 7.38 
元與 8.39 元，分別成長 23.9% 與 28.3% YoY。藍新資訊以核心系統維運掌
握客戶升級需求 ， 並以約九成盈餘發放現金股利 ， 以 10/16 興櫃收盤價 80.8 
元計算，2026 年現金殖利率約 7.2%。採樣資訊服務類股本益比介於 10～20 
倍，考量藍新資訊 2026、2027 年獲利維持雙位數成長，以 2027 年 EPS 為
評價基礎，給予區間中緣的 15 倍本益比，目標價 125 元，評等為買進。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  科技‧美國

Vertiv 
(VRT.N/ VRT US)

Onecore、CDU與 800VDC為三大推升營運加速成長之動能 
重要訊息 
Vertiv  FY3Q26 獲利優於市場共識 21%，公司上修 FY2026 EPS 財測約 7%
至年增 70%，來自資料中心散熱與電源需求升溫， 收盤股價上漲約 16%。 
評論及分析  
FY3Q26 EPS 年增 78%至 2.21 美元、優於市場共識 22%。Vertiv  FY3Q26 
(2026 年 7-9 月)營收年增 61%、季增 32%至 43.1 億美元，主要受惠資料
中心需求升溫，以及 FY2Q26 因供應端問題遞延之營收約 1-1.5 億美元已於
本季認列。FY3Q26 營利率年增 2.9ppts、季增 2.6ppts 至 25.2%，主要受
惠營利率較佳的美洲地區成長較強勁 ；EPS 年增 78%、季增 45%至 2.21 美
元，優於凱基預估 15%、市場共識預估 22%。 
Vertiv 上修 FY2026 EPS 財測約 7%至 7.15 美元。Vertiv  展望 FY4Q26 營
收年增 53%、季增 2%至 44.1 億美元，若還原 FY3Q26 遞延營收之貢獻，
則兩個季度的營收年增率相當； EPS 財測為年增 65%、季增 2%至 2.25 美
元，大致符合市場共識預估。基於資料中心需求升溫， 公司上調 FY2026 營
收約 5%至 146.5 億美元，隱含年增 43%；EPS 財測上修 7%至 7.15 美
元，隱含年增 70%，優於市場共識預估 7%。 
凱基預估 Vertiv  FY2026/2027 EPS 為 7.32/11.89 美元，年增 62%。
Vertiv 在 2026/10 宣布其 2.3MW CDU 取得 Nvidia 認證，供應鏈評估單台售
價將較 1.3MW 提升 50%，每建置 1GW 資料中心的 CDU 產值約達 2 億美
元，而目前僅三家供應商通過認證 ，凱基預期此將有助於公司提升 CDU 供
貨份額，並據此上修 FY2026/FY2027 EPS 自原預估之 6.91/10.94 美元至
7.32/11.89 美元，年增 62%。 
投資建議  
評等維持「增加持股」，目標價 416 美元 (35x FY2027F EPS) 。 
投資風險 
AI 晶片訂單遞延； CSP 廠放緩資本支出增幅；關稅政策變化影響生產成本。  
增加持股‧維持

### Page 2

美國 Vertiv

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Vertiv 營收主要來自資料中心基礎建設解決方案 。Vertiv 業務為提供資料中
心基礎建設解決方案， 營收約 75%來自資料中心客戶。Vertiv 產品組合可分
為硬體 (Products ； FY2Q26 營收占比 80%) 及服務與備品 (Services & 
Spares；20%) 兩大項目。其中  (1) 硬體：提供電源管理、熱管理及整合性
機櫃解決方案，包括交流不斷電供應系統 (UPS)、冷卻系統等；(2) 服務與備
品包含預防性維護、工程和諮詢服務、遠程監控、備件等。FY 2Q26 各地區
營收比重為美洲  68%、亞太地區  19%、歐非中東地區 13%。獲利方面，
美洲基於 AI 資料中心規格要求較高 ，營利率 達 25%-30%、優於公司平
均；歐洲近幾季因關稅成本上揚而使營利率降至 15%-20%；亞太地區因價
格競爭激烈，營利率僅約 10%-15%、低於平均。  
Vertiv 提供完整電源與散熱系統 解決方案，協助客戶建置資料中心 。相較於
散熱或電源廠同業大多僅提供零組件， Vertiv 擁有完整產品線與提供系統建
置的能力；基於下一代 AI 語言模型的運算量與傳輸需求皆顯著提升，使散
熱整合與解決方案的設計難度也隨之向上，讓 Vertiv 相較於僅能提供單一零
組件的廠商更有競爭力。 另外，因應資料中心停機造成損失的問題，Vertiv
也推出 Next Predict ，透過即時遙測與 AI 演算法持續監控 BESS 電池儲能系
統、UPS 與液冷 CDU/泵浦的運作狀態，在異常影響 AI 算力前先行預警，從
而預防損失。Vertiv 資料中心客戶包含 AWS、Meta、Microsoft 、AT&T、
Verizon 、Coreweave 、Oklo，供應模式大多是將電源、散熱與系統等產品
一併提供給客戶，協助其建置資料中心。  
在產品接單上，凱基預期 Vertiv 推出之 OneCore 資料中心建置解決方案將
對整體訂單具有高度貢獻。此解決方案主要是因應伺服器液冷散熱與電源需
求所提供的產品，其採用直接接觸晶片散熱 (Direct -to-Chip Cooling) 技術，
避免晶片在電力故障的幾十秒內因失去冷卻而受損 ，提供建置容量從 10MW
到 1,000MW 的選擇。OneCore 資料中心建置解決方案 之中的 MegaMod ™ 
HDX 主要是針對需要建造一座小型資料中心的客戶所設計，因應數十櫃至數
百櫃的 AI 資料中心建置需求。基於 AI 伺服器機房的液冷散熱管路與電力分
配較為複雜，若由不同供應商安裝可 能會出現漏水或電力供應不足的狀況；
Vertiv 可解決此整合問題，並將安裝時間壓縮在 9-12 個月，相較於大多數
供應商須 18-24 個月而言更為迅速。 目前市場上已有超過 500 家公司有自
建小型資料中心的需求，若以 10MW 方案價格約 8,000 萬美元評估，潛在
市場產值達 400 億美元以上，而觀察目前有提供同等級產品者僅
Schneider( 法)與 Eaton  (美)等同業，使 Vertiv 可望在此領域成為主要供應商
之一， 凱基預期公司 將取得超過 130 億美元訂單，有利於公司
FY2026/FY20 27 營收維持高成長性。  
Vertiv 在 2026/10 宣布其 2.3MW CDU 取得 Nvidia 認證，供應鏈評估單台售
價將較 1.3MW 提升 50%，每建置 1GW 資料中心的 CDU 產值約達 2 億美
元，目前具相同提供能力的供應商僅包含韓國 LG 電子與美國 LiquidStack ，
Vertiv 可望在 CDU 供應鏈提升份額，預期公司可在此市場取得約 20%供貨
份額，並可望貢獻 2027 年營收達 15 億美元。

### Page 3

美國 Vertiv

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

圖 1: Vertiv推出 2.3MW CDU，預估貢獻 2027年營收達 15億美元

資料來源：公司資料；凱基

Nvidia 與 ASIC AI 伺服器陸續導入電源櫃，預計 FY2027 顯著貢獻營收。
基於 Nvidia 與 CSP 廠下世代 AI 伺服器功耗皆顯著提升，客戶對於能處理達
200kW 熱能的液冷散熱需求增加，推升散熱產值提升。另外，因各地政府
對 PUE 規範逐步往 1.5 以下的水準靠攏，而現行氣冷散熱之 PUE 普遍落在
1.7 上下的水準，此限制亦使客戶對散熱方案產生升級需求。凱基 預期在 AI
散熱解決方案升級、MegaMod ™ HDX 營收貢獻逐步增加、以及公司可望在
2.3MW CDU 市場提升份額的帶動下，Vertiv FY2026/FY2027 散熱產品營收
可望達 60.9/92.0 億美元，年增 51%。 
電源產品部分，Vertiv  800VDC 產品目前處於驗證階段 ，預計 2027 年開始
貢獻營收，合作對象包含 hyperscaler 及晶片端夥伴。基於其單價約達 50 萬
美元、預估 2027 年出貨量約落在 1,000- 2,000 櫃上下的水準；公司目前亦
有推出 400VDC 電源櫃，初期以 Meta 和 Microsoft 導入較為積極，預期電
源櫃產品可望貢獻 Vertiv  FY2027 營收達 10 億美元，為其 2027 年電源產品
線主要成長動能，預估 Vertiv FY2026/FY2027 電源產品營收為 36.5/60.7 億
美元，年增 66%。

Vertiv 評等維持「增加持股」，目標價為 416 美元。Vertiv 在資料中心散熱
和電源產品提供的 solution 比大多數同業更完整，營收成長性可望優於產業
平均；凱基預期公司推出的 OneCore 資料中心建置解決方案將拿下超過
130 億美元訂單， 此為支撐公司營運成長之主要動能。另外，公司 在
2026/10 宣布其 2.3MW CDU 取得 Nvidia 認證，供應鏈評估單台售價將較
1.3MW 提升 50%，每建置 1GW 資料中心的 CDU 產值約達 2 億美元，而目
前僅三家供應商通過認證，凱基預期此將有助於公司提升 CDU 供貨份額，
並據此上修 FY2026/FY2027 EPS 自原預估之 6.91/10.94 美元至 7.32/11.89
美元，年增 62%；相較市場共識 EPS 分別為 6.66/9.23 美元，目前股價交易
於約 22/26 倍之 FY2027 凱基與市場共識 EPS 預估。 
Vertiv 過往五年本益比多落於 12-36 倍之間，考量 (1) Vertiv 在電源與散熱
產品之整合能力優於同業表現，並能提供系統級產品，有助公司接單能 力提
升；(2) ASIC 晶片陸續推出且對電源與散熱需求皆提升，可望推升公司未來
兩年獲利成長性優於產業平均；(3) 公司在 2.3MW CDU 與 800VDC 領域皆
為少數供應商之一， FY2027 電源與散熱產品成長性可望進一步提升。綜合
上述原因，預期 Vertiv 本益比將回升至區間上緣之 35 倍上下浮動；基於當
前評價偏低，故維持「增加持股」之 評等，目標價則基於預估 EPS 上修而調
升至 416 美元 (35x FY2027F EPS )。

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: 公司 2025年美洲營收占比達 62% 
Vertiv 前身為 Emerson Network Power ，並於 2016 年開始獨
立運營。公司專注於為資料中心、通訊網路 以及商業與工業環
境提供關鍵基礎設施與服務。產品與服務涵蓋交流與直流電源
管理、不間斷電源系統（ UPS）、電源分配單元（PDU ）、熱
管理解決方案、資料中心基礎設施管理（ DCIM）以及服務
等。2025 年各地區營收比重為美洲 62%、亞太 20%、歐非
中東 18%。

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this

Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

Document 372: Regional Roundup_ Coast-to-coast growth From spending to wages, growth is broad-based across the US, though the South is the clear leader in new business applications. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Regional Roundup_ Coast-to-coast growth From spending to wages, growth is broad-based across the US, though the South is the clear leader in new business applications. 2 min read.pdf

Date: 2026-10-01

13035502     1

Economy 
Regional Roundup: Coast-to-coast growth 
29 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per household is growing at healthy rates across the country. 
Discretionary spending growth has improved markedly relative to the last two years. While the West has led recently and the 
South has lagged somewhat, no region appears to be experiencing a meaningful consumer slowdown. 
• The South has seen softer discretionary retail spending, particularly in home-related durable goods categories. However, the 
region continues to post the strongest after-tax wage growth and has been the largest contributor to growth in new business 
applications nationwide. 
• Business formation is occurring across a wide range of industries, with professional services, retail, healthcare, construction and 
hospitality all accounting for meaningful shares of new business applications. While entrepreneurial activity is broad-based 
across industries nationwide, industry revenue mixes vary considerably by region.

Spending growth is solid across all regions  
Spending growth was strongest in the West in September, while spending growth lagged in the Northeast and South, though not 
by much. Total credit and debit card spending per household increased around 5.6% year-over-year (YoY) in the West, outpacing 
other regions by less than a percentage point, according to Bank of America aggregated card data (Exhibit 1). All regions also 
experienced major improvements in spending growth compared to last year, increasing by over three percentage points in every 
region.   
Spending growth in the Northeast peaked at around 6% YoY in June but remained a solid 4.8% YoY in September. The slowest 
growth was in the South, though spending was still up nearly 4.8% YoY.  
Exhibit 1: Spending growth has mostly improved since the start of the year across all regions 
Total card spending per household by US Census region (three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Discretionary spending growth was also somewhat weaker in the Northeast and South than in the Midwest and West (Exhibit 2). 
However, these two regions have also seen the most improvement in “nice-to-have” spending over the past two years. And here 
spending was also up around 4.8% YoY in both regions, still a very healthy rate of growth.

Durables spending is weighing on discretionary retail spending in the South 
One of the key themes from September in the overall spending data was a broadening in spending growth, particularly in “more 
discretionary” retail categories like clothing, electronics, building materials and non-store/miscellaneous retailers (read more in 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers). Bank of America card data shows that this improvement is occurring across all 
regions.  
However, the South lags, though this is mostly due to declines in spending on home-related durables in, for instance, home 
improvement stores and at furniture retailers. In our view, this could reflect some slowdown in domestic migration to the Sun 
Belt and a corresponding easing in home/moving-related spending (read more in On the move: Renovation over relocation?) 
Exhibit 3: The Midwest and Northeast have seen a higher share of discretionary retail categories with spending growth when compared to the 
South and West

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The South leads in after-tax wage and business application growth 
One reason we are not overly concerned about consumer spending in the South is the strength of after-tax wage growth in the 
region. Bank of America deposit data suggests that after-tax wage growth was strongest there, though only by a slim margin, up 
nearly 4.2% YoY, just ahead of households in the Midwest, and outpacing the West and Northeast by less than half a percentage 
point (Exhibit 4).

Beyond wage income, business ownership offers another potential channel for wealth accumulation. The South has been the 
primary contributor to overall US growth in new business applications so far in 2026, according to US Census Bureau data 
(Exhibit 5).  
While historically only a small share of these applications is likely to become a business employing workers, this surge in 
applications points to a potential pipeline of entrepreneurial activity. For businesses that become established and profitable, 
ownership can provide a pathway to wealth through retained earnings and the accumulation of business equity. Still, the extent 
of that opportunity depends on businesses’ survival, profitability and financing needs (read more in Business Checkpoint: Looking 
into logistics).

Business application growth is widespread across regions and industries 
Looking at 2026 business applications by industry, entrepreneurial activity spans multiple industry groupings (Exhibit 6). 
Professional, scientific, technical and other services account for the largest share, followed by retail trade and the grouping 
comprising healthcare, education services and the arts. Additionally, businesses provide a significant source of income for US 
households. In fact, business revenues accounted for nearly 14% of total pre-tax income, according to the US Census’ 2025 
Survey of Consumer Finances.

Combined, this suggests that potential pathways to business ownership extend not only across various households (read more on 
generational shifts in entrepreneurship in July’s A new generation of business formation) but also across many industries, rather 
than a single sector. 
While business applications show the breadth of prospective entrepreneurial activity nationwide, the industry composition of 
existing business revenue offers a regional lens on business activity. Exhibit 7 shows how revenue was distributed across 
industries within regions according to the 2024 Annual Integrated Economic Survey from the US Census Bureau, highlighting the 
extent to which regional economies are concentrated in particular sectors. 
Although wholesale trade was the sector with the largest share of revenue in each region in the US, there is notable variance in 
economic activity from business revenues across the country. For example, finance and insurance accounted for more than 20% 
of revenue in the Northeast, whereas it’s about half that in the West. Also, manufacturing was only 8.5% of revenue in the 
Northeast, and more than 20% in the Midwest. Overall, the data shows a dynamic national business landscape, with broad 
entrepreneurial activity but distinct regional differences in the industries generating revenue.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend includes total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs. Value grocers is defined using a combination of MCC code and proprietary methods.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 401: Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_.pdf

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4
